# Reviewer guide

Find the code and source tables for 1,356 registered numerical claims below. The current manuscript reference is recorded in [MANUSCRIPT.json](MANUSCRIPT.json). See [change notes](../CHANGELOG.md) for the updates awaiting the next bioRxiv revision. Remaining gaps are listed in each unit's `OPEN_ITEMS.md`.

Generated with `python tools/render_reviewer_guide.py`; edit the unit `NUMBERS.tsv` files, then regenerate. `python tools/render_reviewer_guide.py --check` detects stale documentation. No manuscript text is needed to build this index.

Statuses: `MATCH` records agreement at printed precision; `OPEN` records an unresolved value or trace; `REMOVED_V30`, `REMOVED_V33`, `REMOVED_V36` and `REMOVED_V42` retain claims removed from those versions. Historical v28d statuses are shown separately, including `SUPERSEDED_V33`.

Totals: 1356 registered rows. v42 status: MATCH 1169, OPEN 48, REMOVED_V30 27, REMOVED_V33 66, REMOVED_V36 18, REMOVED_V42 28.

## Check a recorded claim

Find the row, open its code and source table, then run `python paper.py build <unit>`. To verify the printed value and its quote, run `python tools/check_numbers.py --unit <unit> --manuscript-text visible_v42.txt`. The manuscript text is supplied separately. Without it, only registered-value consistency is checked. The default check lists OPEN entries without failing; `--strict` also fails on them. Neither mode discovers manuscript numbers missing from the registered tables.

| 133 | 855 | Standard searches used the Sage v0.14.7 release [17] with K/R cleavage except before proline, two missed cleavages, lengths 5–50 residues and masses 500–5,000 Da. Precursor and fragment tolerances were ±20 ppm. Carbamidomethylation of cysteine was fixed and methionine oxidation variable, with at most two variable modifications. Searches allowed isotope errors −1 to +3, required four matched peaks and reported one peptide–spectrum match (PSM) per spectrum unless specified. Sage generated terminal-preserving reversed decoys. FastaLake FASTAs contained selected reservoir proteins without an appended contaminant library. Five truncated PREDICT mzML inputs were replaced by validated RAW reconversions; conversion versions and analysis-specific exceptions are given in Supplementary Methods 4 and 7. | 5–50 | 5–50 | text_statistics/supplementary_methods_8_11/stats.py:stat_nonspecific | source_data/supplementary_methods_8_11/nonspecific_search_summary.tsv | MATCH | MATCH | supplementary_methods_8_11 |

| 269 | 485 | Supplementary Table 5 \| Shuffled-query controls in all 726 acquisitions and two seeds. | 308 | 67 | De novo predictions carry residual order ambiguity that AlphaNovo records as bags, and near-isobaric composition ambiguity that a swap table can enumerate. We tested whether admitting either at the lookup step improved the final identification list. Over 717 acquisitions (15 CAMPI, 50 healthy stool, 306 PREDICT and 346 CapScan; nine acquisitions without a reproducible exact baseline were excluded), admitting recorded bags added 67,875 peptide–acquisition events and removed 48,358 (net +19,517, positive in 483 of 717). Restricting the bag route to predictions with at most 8, 4 or 2 orderings gave net changes of +14,368, +9,210 and −216 (positive in 476, 455 and 405 acquisitions). Admitting mass-equivalent substitutions from the full swap table added 430,798 and removed 568,964 events (net −138,166, positive in 53 of 717). Across all 726 acquisitions and two seeds, shuffled-query controls yielded 170,441 peptide–acquisition–seed observations through the mass route, and the exact and bag routes accepted peptides in a single acquisition–seed, where a shuffled query selected abundant human titin (Supplementary Table 5). The separate matched comparison and fragment-ion check are detailed below and in Supplementary Fig. 2e,f. | 717 | 717 | figures/SFig02/current.py:numbers[acquisitions] | source_data/SFig02/bags_bounded717_per_acquisition.tsv;source_data/SFig02/fragment717_per_acquisition.tsv | MATCH | MATCH | SFig02 |

| 343 | 657 | Functional annotations were indexed by full-length protein sequence, with I and L treated as equivalent, and combined from four sources. The first was eggNOG-mapper 2.1.12 in DIAMOND mode [8,9] (eggNOG database 5.0.2, DIAMOND 2.0.11, default settings) run on the searched proteins of each study in August and September 2026. The second was the MGnify per-genome eggNOG tables of the UHGG v2.0 species catalog (4,744 species representatives, with no record of the eggNOG-mapper version), joined by UHGG gene identifier and used only when DIAMOND aligned the protein to the annotation’s seed ortholog with at least half of the reported bitscore, because these tables list gene identifiers without sequences. The third comprised earlier eggNOG-mapper 2.1.12 runs on GMGC and GMSC catalog proteins, joined after sequence verification, for which the eggNOG database version was not recorded. The fourth was an eggNOG-mapper 2.1.12 run on 17,935 otherwise unannotated CAMPI reference proteins. Of 9,368,708 MGnify annotations, 372,277 (4.0%) failed this check, including 191,565 (2.0%) with no alignment. Most failures involved proteins shorter than half the seed ortholog or a seed shared with the adjacent gene. Values before and after this check are provided in Source Data. A protein kept every distinct annotation set from these sources. MGnify tables supplied annotations for 88–95% of compatible proteins, depending on the study. Each accepted LFQ feature’s canonical peptide defined its compatible proteins in the searched-target union plus the full local UHGG catalog. The five CAMPI arms shared one union. Distinct terms from all compatible proteins were collected at the requested level: KEGG orthologs matching K\d{5}, bacterial/archaeal scoped orthologous-group tokens, or pathways matching map\d{5}, excluding map011 global maps but retaining map012 overview maps. The feature intensity was divided equally among distinct eligible terms. Repeated support for the same term did not increase its share, and compatible proteins without an annotation did not remove terms supported by other proteins. Features without an eligible term remained functionally unassigned. In profiles excluding human-assigned signal, human-assigned features were withheld as a separate category. Allocated, unassigned and withheld intensity was checked against input intensity for every acquisition and level. Microbial single-OG profiles used only annotation sets that consisted of a single bacterial or archaeal scoped OG. A peptide could still have several eligible protein/term matches. The unassigned and withheld remainders were reported but excluded from functional distance calculations. As a sensitivity analysis, we allocated intensity only from features with a single eligible term, only from features whose compatible proteins were all annotated, or both. In profiles excluding human-assigned signal, these rules allocated a median of 7–47% instead of 61–81% of total intensity, depending on cohort and level. All 15 CapScan participants remained closer to their own stool (P=1 × 10⁻⁵), and PREDICT protein profile distances remained correlated with DNA, RNA and metabolite distances (ρ=0.16–0.64 against 0.16–0.51 with equal allocation, all P≤0.025), but their correlations with human-assigned protein profile distances fell from 0.43–0.51 to 0.28–0.36 (P≤0.002). In healthy stool, KO correlations still decreased from technical replicates (r=0.89–0.90) to different individuals, but the inter-individual value fell from 0.81 to 0.68–0.71, below the within-donor value, so the equal-allocation value for this design partly reflects terms shared through multi-term allocation. | 7 | 6.6199416613052 | tools/v42_values.py:numbers[allocation_restricted_min] | source_data/v42_updates/allocation_medians.tsv | MATCH | MATCH | supplementary_methods_1_5 |

The first was eggNOG-mapper 2.1.12 in DIAMOND mode [8,9] (eggNOG database 5.0.2, DIAMOND 2.0.11, default settings) run on the searched proteins of each study in August and September 2026. The second was the MGnify per-genome eggNOG tables of the UHGG v2.0 species catalog (4,744 species representatives, with no record of the eggNOG-mapper version), joined by UHGG gene identifier and used only when DIAMOND aligned the protein to the annotation’s seed ortholog with at least half of the reported bitscore, because these tables list gene identifiers without sequences. Most failures involved proteins shorter than half the seed ortholog or a seed shared with the adjacent gene. Values before and after this check are provided in Source Data. A protein kept every distinct annotation set from these sources. MGnify tables supplied annotations for 88–95% of compatible proteins, depending on the study. Each accepted LFQ feature’s canonical peptide defined its compatible proteins in the searched-target union plus the full local UHGG catalog. The five CAMPI arms shared one union. Distinct terms from all compatible proteins were collected at the requested level: KEGG orthologs matching K\d{5}, bacterial/archaeal scoped orthologous-group tokens, or pathways matching map\d{5}, excluding map011 global maps but retaining map012 overview maps. The feature intensity was divided equally among distinct eligible terms. Repeated support for the same term did not increase its share, and compatible proteins without an annotation did not remove terms supported by other proteins. Features without an eligible term remained functionally unassigned. In profiles excluding human-assigned signal, human-assigned features were withheld as a separate category. Allocated, unassigned and withheld intensity was checked against input intensity for every acquisition and level. Microbial single-OG profiles used only annotation sets that consisted of a single bacterial or archaeal scoped OG. A peptide could still have several eligible protein/term matches. The unassigned and withheld remainders were reported but excluded from functional distance calculations. As a sensitivity analysis, we allocated intensity only from features with a single eligible term, only from features whose compatible proteins were all annotated, or both. In profiles excluding human-assigned signal, these rules allocated a median of 7–47% instead of 61–81% of total intensity, depending on cohort and level. All 15 CapScan participants remained closer to their own stool (P=1 × 10⁻⁵), and PREDICT protein profile distances remained correlated with DNA, RNA and metabolite distances (ρ=0.16–0.64 against 0.16–0.51 with equal allocation, all P≤0.025), but their correlations with human-assigned protein profile distances fell from 0.43–0.51 to 0.28–0.36 (P≤0.002). In healthy stool, KO correlations still decreased from technical replicates (r=0.89–0.90) to different individuals, but the inter-individual value fell from 0.81 to 0.68–0.71, below the within-donor value, so the equal-allocation value for this design partly reflects terms shared through multi-term allocation. | 0.025 | 0.025 | tools/v42_values.py:numbers[other_p_max] | source_data/v42_updates/allocation_predict.tsv | MATCH | MATCH | supplementary_methods_1_5 |

| 343 | 657 | Functional annotations were indexed by full-length protein sequence, with I and L treated as equivalent, and combined from four sources. The first was eggNOG-mapper 2.1.12 in DIAMOND mode [8,9] (eggNOG database 5.0.2, DIAMOND 2.0.11, default settings) run on the searched proteins of each study in August and September 2026. The second was the MGnify per-genome eggNOG tables of the UHGG v2.0 species catalog (4,744 species representatives, with no record of the eggNOG-mapper version), joined by UHGG gene identifier and used only when DIAMOND aligned the protein to the annotation’s seed ortholog with at least half of the reported bitscore, because these tables list gene identifiers without sequences. The third comprised earlier eggNOG-mapper 2.1.12 runs on GMGC and GMSC catalog proteins, joined after sequence verification, for which the eggNOG database version was not recorded. The fourth was an eggNOG-mapper 2.1.12 run on 17,935 otherwise unannotated CAMPI reference proteins. Of 9,368,708 MGnify annotations, 372,277 (4.0%) failed this check, including 191,565 (2.0%) with no alignment. Most failures involved proteins shorter than half the seed ortholog or a seed shared with the adjacent gene. Values before and after this check are provided in Source Data. A protein kept every distinct annotation set from these sources. MGnify tables supplied annotations for 88–95% of compatible proteins, depending on the study. Each accepted LFQ feature’s canonical peptide defined its compatible proteins in the searched-target union plus the full local UHGG catalog. The five CAMPI arms shared one union. Distinct terms from all compatible proteins were collected at the requested level: KEGG orthologs matching K\d{5}, bacterial/archaeal scoped orthologous-group tokens, or pathways matching map\d{5}, excluding map011 global maps but retaining map012 overview maps. The feature intensity was divided equally among distinct eligible terms. Repeated support for the same term did not increase its share, and compatible proteins without an annotation did not remove terms supported by other proteins. Features without an eligible term remained functionally unassigned. In profiles excluding human-assigned signal, human-assigned features were withheld as a separate category. Allocated, unassigned and withheld intensity was checked against input intensity for every acquisition and level. Microbial single-OG profiles used only annotation sets that consisted of a single bacterial or archaeal scoped OG. A peptide could still have several eligible protein/term matches. The unassigned and withheld remainders were reported but excluded from functional distance calculations. As a sensitivity analysis, we allocated intensity only from features with a single eligible term, only from features whose compatible proteins were all annotated, or both. In profiles excluding human-assigned signal, these rules allocated a median of 7–47% instead of 61–81% of total intensity, depending on cohort and level. All 15 CapScan participants remained closer to their own stool (P=1 × 10⁻⁵), and PREDICT protein profile distances remained correlated with DNA, RNA and metabolite distances (ρ=0.16–0.64 against 0.16–0.51 with equal allocation, all P≤0.025), but their correlations with human-assigned protein profile distances fell from 0.43–0.51 to 0.28–0.36 (P≤0.002). In healthy stool, KO correlations still decreased from technical replicates (r=0.89–0.90) to different individuals, but the inter-individual value fell from 0.81 to 0.68–0.71, below the within-donor value, so the equal-allocation value for this design partly reflects terms shared through multi-term allocation. | 0.68 | 0.6796024802995962 | tools/v42_values.py:numbers[inter_restricted_min] | source_data/v42_updates/allocation_hstool.tsv | MATCH | MATCH | supplementary_methods_1_5 |

| 381 | 733 | (20,000 bootstrap resamples) | 20,000 | 20000 | text_statistics/supplementary_methods_6_7/stats.py:stat_simulation | source_data/supplementary_methods_6_7/simulation_design.tsv | MATCH | MATCH | supplementary_methods_6_7 |
| 381 | 733 | Whole-procedure error was counted directly only in a restricted simulation in which generating peptide sequences were known. AlphaPeptDeep-predicted spectra for 2,048 generating peptides per acquisition were noise-injected to create 12 baseline and 12 stress mixtures. Each mixture was searched against a fixed reference, a database selected from the measurement being searched and a database selected from an independently noised replicate. Generating peptides were 7–30 residues and originated from an approximately 30,000-protein CAMPI reference. Searches accepted peptides of 5–50 residues. For each target canonical-peptide list passing peptide q≤0.01, false discoveries were counted directly against the known generating sequences. The false-discovery proportion was the number of false discoveries divided by the larger of the total number of discoveries and one. Means and 95% bootstrap intervals were calculated over whole mixtures, separately for the 12 units in each regime (20,000 bootstrap resamples). These simulations are controlled stress tests. All 72 ordinary-search outcomes were retained and form a defined subset of 288 total simulation outcomes, which also include entrapment-control searches. Mean false-discovery proportions at the nominal 1% peptide threshold were 1.25% for the fixed-reference search, 1.06% with selection from the replicate and 1.01% with selection from the measurement being searched under baseline conditions, and 1.50%, 1.20% and 1.91%, respectively, under stress conditions. Stress conditions combined increased spectral noise with greater reference incompleteness: 50% of generating peptides were deliberately absent from the search reference, compared with 25% at baseline. Under stress, selection from the measurement being searched increased the proportion by 0.71 percentage points relative to selection from the replicate (95% bootstrap interval, 0.35–1.12) and by 0.41 percentage points relative to the fixed-reference search. | 72 | 72 | text_statistics/supplementary_methods_6_7/stats.py:stat_simulation | source_data/supplementary_methods_6_7/simulation_all_outcomes.tsv | MATCH | MATCH | supplementary_methods_6_7 |

| 398 | 765 | The three healthy-stool search designs are defined in Methods. Peptide-count comparisons required a rank-one target peptide accepted at peptide q≤0.01 in the corresponding acquisition. For per-acquisition confidence, the joint PSMs were divided by acquisition. Within each acquisition, the best discriminant score was taken for each modified peptide sequence and label, q was calculated as the number of decoys divided by the number of targets at or above each score and made monotone, and rank-one target peptides with q≤0.01 were accepted. Applied once across all 58 acquisitions together, the same estimator gave a median gain of 32.0%, compared with 32.9% from Sage, for peptides of 5–50 residues (34.2% from Sage at 9–50 residues). Across 1,000 permutations of target and decoy labels within each acquisition, it accepted at most 47 peptides in any acquisition (median per-permutation maximum 15). With one added to the decoy count, it accepted none in any permutation. Quantified features were then gated by these per-acquisition identifications in the same way as for study-level confidence. Quantitative comparisons likewise required a positive finite LFQ feature with an accepted canonical peptide in that acquisition. | 47 | 47 | tools/v42_values.py:numbers[null_max] | source_data/v42_updates/label_permutations.tsv | MATCH | MATCH | supplementary_methods_6_7 |

| 402 | 773 | Fold membership was assigned deterministically from the native scan number (Methods); the exact fold key is given in the code and Source Data. Folds held 9.8–10.3% of MS2 spectra across all 591 acquisitions. For each cohort and fold, out-of-fold prediction tables of all 306 PREDICT and 347 CapScan acquisitions were written and used to rebuild the cohort evidence cache from the original reservoir with the unchanged top-30% eligibility, 9–50-residue lookup and I/L equivalence. Per-acquisition selection used selection parsimony on the acquisition’s out-of-fold predictions. No prediction from a fold-k spectrum entered the fold-k selection. FastaLake, all-prediction and matched metagenome databases were searched with Sage v0.14.7 and the archived per-acquisition configuration, changing only database, input and output paths. | 9.8 | 9.8202 | tools/v42_values.py:numbers[fold_min] | source_data/v42_updates/fold_fractions.tsv | MATCH | MATCH | supplementary_methods_6_7 |

Folds held 9.8–10.3% of MS2 spectra across all 591 acquisitions. For each cohort and fold, out-of-fold prediction tables of all 306 PREDICT and 347 CapScan acquisitions were written and used to rebuild the cohort evidence cache from the original reservoir with the unchanged top-30% eligibility, 9–50-residue lookup and I/L equivalence. Per-acquisition selection used selection parsimony on the acquisition’s out-of-fold predictions. No prediction from a fold-k spectrum entered the fold-k selection. FastaLake, all-prediction and matched metagenome databases were searched with Sage v0.14.7 and the archived per-acquisition configuration, changing only database, input and output paths. | 591 | 591 | tools/v42_values.py:numbers[fold_acquisitions] | source_data/v42_updates/fold_fractions.tsv | MATCH | MATCH | supplementary_methods_6_7 |

| 417 | 803 | Exploratory protein-based EDS prediction used the fixed 85-patient roster, one observation per patient and the peptide-based OG profiles, with quantified-group counts under direct peptide assignment as the depth covariate. OG features with positive intensity in at least 10% of training observations were transformed to square-root relative abundance. Models used protein features alone, log10 protein-group depth plus human-assigned intensity fraction alone, or both. Baseline variables were standardized using training-fold means and population standard deviations. Ridge penalties 0.01, 0.1, 1, 10 and 100 were selected by mean squared error in three inner folds within each of five shuffled patient-separated outer folds. Three outer split seeds (20260915–20260917) were retained. A separate distance score averaged the five nearest training profiles among those in the training EDS lower quartile. All filtering, transformation estimates, penalty choices and low-EDS reference selection were confined to training data. For each of the three outer split seeds, 199 label permutations repeated feature filtering, model selection, low-EDS reference selection and held-out prediction. | 199 | 199 | tools/v42_values.py:numbers[eds_permutations] | source_data/v42_updates/eds_validation.tsv;source_data/v42_updates/eds_permutations.tsv | MATCH | MATCH | supplementary_methods_8_11 |
| 418 | 805 | Two-sided Spearman permutation probabilities used the plus-one correction and Benjamini–Hochberg adjustment across the four model tests. Other split seeds assessed sensitivity. Two thousand patient resamples (seed 20260918), each refitting the complete nested procedure, provided 95% intervals for correlation and baseline-minus-model mean absolute error. All held-out predictions, folds, model metrics, selected penalties, permutation outcomes and depth/host controls are supplied. | Two thousand | 2000 | tools/v42_values.py:numbers[eds_bootstraps] | source_data/v42_updates/eds_validation.tsv | MATCH | MATCH | supplementary_methods_8_11 |
